  provider call. This is P1 because invalid model output must terminalize deterministically.

  failed with zero candidates/evidence and no second call. Migration 0107 and all remote/UAT,

## Completed correction gate — 69B.5I-E-A2

- Fresh independent rereview returned
  `AUDIT_69B5IEB2=FAIL P0=0 P1=2 P2=0 can_deploy_uat=false`.
- P1 #1 is causal: TypeScript sorts the final exception-reason set, but migration 0105
  appends `relation_target_unresolved` after the already sorted base reasons and then
  compares JSON arrays exactly. A typed relation with both `semantic_collision` and an
  unresolved target is a valid deterministic exception in TypeScript but fails the SQL
  authority check and aborts the terminal cohort.
- P1 #2 is contractual: runtime review facets return only states present in the rows,
  while the closed OpenAPI requires `ready`, `exception` and `resolved` on every response.
  The current PostgreSQL fixture codifies the sparse invalid response and the UI masks it
  with client-side zero defaults.
- E-A2 is local-only. Canonicalize the SQL final reason set exactly like TypeScript and
  return an explicit closed three-key review-state facet with zeros. Add failing-before /
  passing-after coverage, including the mixed collision+missing-target terminal cohort,
  then rerun disposable PostgreSQL 0000→0105 and the fake-provider terminal path.
- Migration 0105 remains uncommitted and unapplied. Preview/UAT remains on 0104 at exact
  `0/69/11/0/0`, archived `1`, unresolved `0`, blockers `0`. Deployment remains closed.

  from missing environment and source order rather than an injected factory seam with
  measured zero calls. Its callback matcher also accepts arbitrary text between the
  pinned prefix and Authentication-flow suffix, so a noncanonical 500 can become a 303.
- A parallel security review added three P2s: forbidden exact auth paths with query or
  fragment are compared as full strings; guards still classify one token read and
  authenticate with another; and failed/ambiguous terminal concurrency lacks explicit
  waiter/replay coverage. The manifest's historical failing-before count is not treated
  as audited evidence because it lacks a hashed red-run artifact.
- Gate 69A.7Y2C is local-only. It must introduce exact injected route factories, one
  anchored callback matcher, pathname-based forbidden-root checks, a single-snapshot
  lifecycle/authentication resolver and terminal-state concurrency tests. It authorizes
  no remotes, commit/push, deployment, real refresh, providers, Advisor or product writes.

- Gate 69A.7Y3 is paused before commit, UAT configuration, push or deployment. Two
  independent reviews proved three local P1 gaps in the 69A.7Y2 slice. First, only the
  refresh owner request receives Kinde's request-local `Set-Cookie` mutations, while a
  waiter that observes Redis `rotated` currently redirects as if it possessed those
  cookies. If the owner response is delayed or lost, the waiter can loop on the retained
  terminal record. Second, the pinned Kinde callback handler catches `State not found`
  internally and returns HTTP 500, so the outer route `catch` is unreachable for the
  exact failure it claims to repair.
- Gate 69A.7Y2B is local-only and must prove both repairs with route/SDK-shaped tests and
  per-response cookie-delivery modeling. Non-owner waiters may never treat Redis
  `rotated` as proof that their response committed cookies. The callback route must
  detect the pinned SDK's returned terminal response without weakening other callback
  failures. Third, the concrete refresh route currently constructs Kinde/config/Redis
  before the pure handler rejects invalid Sec-Fetch navigation, so cross-site requests
  do not provably fail before those boundaries. Exact `/api` and `/api/auth` return
  targets must also fail closed.
- This gate authorizes no remote, UAT configuration, commit, push, deployment, provider,
  Advisor, real Kinde rotation, product-data write, publication or serving change.
